The repo contains a README and a zip file. Let me inspect the contents and git state.
思考过程
I'm not sure unzip is installed, so let me check for it first.

I'm looking at the repo structure — it's a README plus a zipped Vite React app displaying static JSON data files, with a claimed daily update that isn't actually automated. The user wants me to add real automation with an LLM-driven update pipeline and a user-configurable schedule, so I'm planning to first unzip and extract the app into the repo itself.

Next I need to design a pipeline that collects job listings (with pluggable/mock sources since scraping sites like 51job or Boss isn't reliable), feeds them through a unified LLM interface (supporting OpenAI-compatible, Anthropic, DeepSeek, Kimi, or custom endpoints) to normalize and extract insights, then writes the results into the public/data JSON files. For scheduling, I'm thinking a config file where users set their preferred run time, combined with a GitHub Actions workflow — since GH Actions cron is static, I could have the workflow run hourly and have the pipeline itself check the config to decide whether it's actually time to execute.

I also want to provide a CLI supporting single runs, a scheduler daemon mode, config validation, and LLM connection testing, plus optional Docker support. On the frontend side, I want to surface a "last updated" timestamp and maybe a small LLM insights section, but I need to be careful not to break the existing large App.tsx file — I should check how it currently loads data before deciding how to integrate this.

Before diving in, I should check what's available in the environment — Node, Python, network/pip access — since that affects whether the automation backend is best written in Python (leveraging APScheduler, croniter, requests) or kept in TypeScript/Node to avoid introducing a second runtime. I'll extract the project and look at the key files first to decide.

I'm interpreting "增加LLM自动化调用接口" broadly: both a provider-agnostic LLM client abstraction and an HTTP control API (endpoints for triggering analysis, scheduling, and manual runs) so the whole thing functions as a real automation project rather than just a script.

Sketching out project structure...

Rounding out the structure with scheduler, API, and storage modules, plus tests and deployment configs. Given dependency risk, I'm leaning toward a stdlib-only fallback -- using urllib for HTTP, a custom cron parser, and a minimal HTTP server -- so the project stays safe and functional even if pip installs fail.



Pip and network access work fine, and Node 22 is available, but I notice the workspace directory shows read-only permissions with an odd zero size, which looks like it could be a fuse mount. I should just test writing to confirm.

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Writable and both Python and Node are available. Let me extract the zip to understand the existing app and its data shapes.
思考过程
Let me check the data shapes and see how App.tsx loads its data.



I want to check App.tsx for how it loads data and see what keys remain in stats.json, so I'll search for fetch calls and related interfaces.



Now I have a clearer picture of the repo — it's a static React dashboard with hand-generated data claiming daily updates but lacking real automation. I'm planning to restructure it into an automation project with a unified LLM invocation interface plus HTTP API, and support for user-customizable scheduling, starting by extracting the frontend into its own `web/` directory.

I'm sketching out an `automation/` Python package called `jobsinsight`, with a config module supporting YAML/JSON and env var expansion, an `llm/` subpackage abstracting providers like OpenAI-compatible APIs, Anthropic, and a mock provider for offline testing, plus a registry and high-level client with retries and JSON-schema completion. I'm also designing a `collectors/` layer with a protocol for job postings and fixture/HTTP-based collector implementations for real scraping sources.

Since real platform scraping is fragile and blocked, I'm going with a generic HTML collector that fetches configured URLs and passes raw text to the LLM for extraction, defaulting to a fixture collector so the pipeline still works offline, and being upfront in the README about needing user-provided endpoints. I'm outlining `pipeline.py` to collect, dedupe, enrich via LLM, aggregate, and write the JSON outputs the frontend expects, supporting dry runs and incremental diffs, alongside an `analysis.py` for pure stats aggregation and a `scheduler.py` for user-defined run times via daily times or cron expressions.

Now I'm sketching `server.py` as a stdlib-only HTTP control API exposing health, schedule, run triggers, run history, and LLM chat/analyze endpoints while serving the static frontend build, plus `cli.py` for run-once, schedule, serve, config, and diagnostic commands, and starting on `state.py`/`store.py` for persistence.

I'm planning the storage layer with atomic JSON writes and run history under `automation/state/`, a pytest suite covering cron parsing, scheduler logic, analysis, LLM client mocking, and end-to-end pipeline runs, a GitHub Actions workflow with hourly cron and dispatch triggers that commits updated data plus a sync-schedule command, Docker/compose setup for the daemon, and a frontend automation panel tying the "每日更新" button into the new API with a static fallback.

I need to be careful editing the large App.tsx file — I'll add a small dedicated section component and hook rather than modifying it heavily, plus type the insights.json shape. Given the scope, I'll prioritize the automation backend with solid tests, docs, and CI, treating frontend integration as secondary, and I'm now weighing config format: PyYAML adds a dependency, so I'm leaning toward TOML since Python 3.11's built-in tomllib keeps things dependency-free.

For persisting runtime schedule updates, since tomllib is read-only, I'll layer a JSON override file (state/schedule_override.json) on top of the base config instead of writing TOML. So the plan is config.toml or config.json as the base, optional PyYAML if installed, and JSON-based runtime overrides — time to set up the branch and start moving files.

耗时 4704 ms

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now the core of the work: the automation package. Starting with the LLM interface layer.
思考过程
Now I'm setting up an HTTP transport helper using the standard urllib library, then moving on to the provider implementations.

I'm designing a mock provider for offline runs that inspects the prompt for a task tag like "#task:<name>" and delegates to a registered canned generator to produce plausible JSON responses.

Really though, the cleaner path is to let each LLM-driven step in the pipeline define its own heuristic fallback in a separate `heuristics.py`, so it works with zero API key. The `LLMEnricher` has an `enabled` flag, and when the LLM is disabled or fails, it falls back to the heuristic logic that actually produces skills/level/summary offline, with tests covering both paths.

For the mock provider itself, I want it smart enough to route by task tag found in the prompt and call the right heuristic — so `provider="mock"` exercises the real pipeline end-to-end with plausible data instead of empty stubs. Two tasks matter here: `enrich_job`, which normalizes a raw posting into salary range, skills, level, experience bucket, education, summary, relevance score, and category; and `daily_insight`, which turns aggregated stats into a headline, summary, bullets, hot skills, and advice for the frontend's insights.json. The mock handlers for each would derive their output from the heuristics module and the stats payload respectively.

To pull the payload out of a prompt, I'm planning a brace-matching JSON extractor that both the mock provider and a general `complete_json` repair path can reuse. My build order is: a json_utils module with that extractor, then the mock provider (importing heuristics, checking for circular import issues — should be fine since heuristics has no llm dependency), then the client with retry/backoff and usage accounting plus a `chat_json` convenience method, then the registry. I'll start with the heuristics module since both mock and the enricher's fallback path depend on it.

Now I'm defining the data model: a `RawPosting` dataclass capturing source fields like source_id, platform, title, company, city, and optional text fields for salary/experience/education/description, plus a `Job` dataclass matching the frontend schema with fields like salary_min/max, experience, education, skills, and extra optional fields like url, relevance, category, and ai_enriched — since the TS side parses fetched data loosely, extra fields won't break anything.

distinct handling per error\n+        detail = exc.read().decode(\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")[:800]\n+        raise LLMTransportError(\n+            f\"HTTP {exc.code} from {url}: {detail}\",\n+            status=exc.code,\n+            retryable=exc.code in RETRYABLE_STATUS,\n+        ) from exc\n+    except urllib.error.URLError as exc:\n+        raise LLMTransportError(f\"cannot reach {url}: {exc.reason}\") from exc\n+    except TimeoutError as exc:\n+        raise LLMTransportError(f\"timeout after {timeout}s calling {url}\") from exc\n+\n+    try:\n+        parsed = json.loads(raw)\n+    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:\n+        raise LLMTransportError(f\"non-JSON response from {url}: {raw[:400]}\", retryable=False) from exc\n+\n+    if not isinstance(parsed, dict):\n+        raise LLMTransportError(f\"unexpected response shape from {url}: {type(parsed).__name__}\", retryable=False)\n+    return parsed",

耗时 450 ms

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now the JSON helpers, mock provider, client with retries, and provider registry.
思考过程
Now I need to build the registry that constructs a provider from config, plus the module's exports. To avoid a circular import between config.py and llm, I'll have the registry accept a `from_settings` method that only references the `LLMSettings` dataclass under `TYPE_CHECKING`, so config.py never actually imports the llm module at runtime.

I'll also allow custom provider registration via a `register_provider(name, factory)` function, and support resolving API keys from environment variables through either an `${OPENAI_API_KEY}`-style interpolation or an explicit `api_key_env` field.

I'm designing the schedule config schema, letting users choose daily times, cron expressions, fixed intervals, or manual runs, plus timezone and catch-up handling for missed windows.

For loading, I'm planning support for TOML/JSON/YAML with environment variable expansion and JOBSINSIGHT_* overrides, plus a runtime overrides file that persists API changes to the schedule.

Then I'm sketching out the core dataclasses: LLM settings (provider, retries, rate limits, task configs), schedule settings, per-collector source settings, output settings, server settings, and the top-level Config that ties them together, followed by writing config.py with load and serialization functions.

Before finishing config validation, I need a cron parser since ScheduleSettings depends on it. I'm designing a 5-field cron parser supporting wildcards, steps, ranges, and lists, then computing the next matching datetime efficiently by stepping through days, hours, and minutes hierarchically rather than brute-forcing every minute.

耗时 529 ms

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now the state store and the scheduler.
思考过程
The next_run logic for Scheduler feels tangled, so I'm working through the interval mode case: if last_run_at is very stale relative to the interval, computing next_run from that stale timestamp would land in the past, which should just trigger an immediate run rather than an error.

I'm simplifying next_run to compute from last_run_at when in interval mode (falling back to now if unset), and otherwise just from the current clock. I'm also cleaning up is_due to drop the redundant None check since missed_runs already handles that case naturally, just checking whether the generator yields anything.

耗时 377 ms

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now the collectors that feed the pipeline.
思考过程
I'm planning the collectors: a fixture-based one that reads seed JSON data (which I could generate from the existing jobs.json for realistic demo content), plus a generic JSON API collector for fetching and extracting data from HTTP endpoints.

For real scraping, I want an HTML+LLM collector that fetches pages, strips them to text, and hands chunks to an LLM for structured extraction — this needs an injected LLM client. I'm restructuring the factory to pass a context object carrying the project root and LLM client, rather than bare parameters, and updating base.py to match this cleaner design.

I'm designing the enricher to batch job postings into a single LLM call with fallback heuristics and value clamping, but I realize the mock provider only handles single-posting payloads, so I need to add a batch-oriented handler for enrichment.

I'm defining the fields the LLM returns per posting—salary range, experience, education, job level, skills, category, summary, relevance—with validation that falls back to heuristics when values are missing or invalid.

Then I'm mapping out the analysis module's stats schema, matching the frontend's expected structure: job counts, platform/city/skill breakdowns, experience buckets, and salary distributions with top city/title details, working through how to bucket salaries reasonably including edge cases below the minimum range.

I'm also planning the new insights.json file structure alongside the existing jobs.json and stats.json, plus keeping headhunters.json and agencies.json static while adding LLM-generated insights with headlines, highlights, hot skills, and run metadata.

Now I'll start writing analysis.py to implement all of this.

(\"35-50K\", 35, 50),\n+    (\"50-70K\", 50, 70),\n+    (\"70-100K\", 70, 100),\n+    (\"100K+\", 100, float(\"inf\")),\n+)\n+\n+\n+def salary_bucket(avg_salary: float) -> str | None:\n+    if avg_salary <= 0:\n+        return None\n+    for label, low, high in SALARY_BUCKETS:\n+        if low <= avg_salary < high:\n+            return label\n+    return SALARY_BUCKETS[-1][0]\n+\n+\n+def _count_avg_stats(jobs, \"experience\")\n+    ordered = {bucket: stats[bucket] for bucket in EXPERIENCE_BUCKETS if bucket in stats}\n+    ordered.update({key: value for key, value in stats.items() if key not in ordered})\n+    return ordered\n+\n+\n+def skill_stats(jobs: Iterable[Job], *, limit: int = 24) -> dict[str, int]:\n+    counter: Counter[str] = Counter()\n+    for job in jobs:\n+        counter.update({skill for skill in job.skills if skill})\n+    return dict(counter.most_common(limit))\n+\n+\n+def category_stats(jobs: Iterable[Job], *, limit: int = 12) -> dict[str, int]:\n+    counter: Counter[str] = Counter(job.category or \"其他\" for job in jobs)\n+    return dict(counter.most_common(limit))\n+\n+\n+def salary_distribution(jobs: Sequence[Job]) -> tuple[dict[str, int], grouped[bucket].append(job)\n+\n+    distribution: dict[str, int] = {}\n+    detail: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}\n+    for label, _, _ in SALARY_BUCKETS:\n+        bucket_jobs = \"salary_distribution\": distribution,\n+        \"salary_distribution_detail\": distribution_detail,\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def diff_jobs(previous: Sequence[dict[str, Any]], current: Sequence[Job]) -> RunDiff:\n+    \"\"\"Compare against the previous snapshot to fill the \"今日\" counters.\"\"\"\n+\n+    def key_of(entry: Any) -> str:\n+        if isinstance(entry, dict):\n+            return \"updated\"\n+        else:\n+            diff.unchanged_jobs += 1\n+            job.update_date = str(old.get(\"update_date\") or job.update_date)\n+\n+    diff.deleted_jobs = 助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now the pipeline that ties collection, enrichment, analysis and output together.
思考过程
I'm finalizing the run status logic to mark failure when postings are empty and errors occurred, then moving on to build the HTTP API server with endpoints for health, status, and schedule management, along with the CLI and package entry points.

I'm mapping out the remaining routes: triggering and querying runs, LLM chat and grounded Q&A passthroughs, redacted config access, serving data files, and static asset delivery for the web dashboard. I also need bearer-token auth on write operations and a lock to serialize runs, with async runs executing in a worker thread.

耗时 533 ms

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now the HTTP control API — this is what makes the automation callable at runtime.
思考过程
Directly accessing the private wakeup member isn't ideal—I should add a public `refresh()` method on the Scheduler instead and use that. The ScheduleSettings update looks fine since max_run_seconds is a valid field. I'll add that refresh method to the scheduler now.

I realize I need to distinguish whether the wait woke up due to a timeout versus an actual event, since `wait()` returns True if set and False if it timed out—I should use that return value to decide whether to just recompute the delay and loop again rather than firing a schedule event.

This module additionally rewrites the cron line to\n+the closest UTC equivalent of the configured schedule, so the workflow fires\n+near the requested time instead of on an unrelated cadence.\n+\"\"\"\n+\n+from __future__ import annotations\n+\n+import re\n+from dataclasses import dataclass\n+from pathlib import Path\n+\n+from .config import ScheduleSettings\n+from .cron import parse_cron\n+\n+_CRON_LINE = re.compile(r\"^(?P<indent>\\s*-\\s*cron:\\s*)(?P<quote>['\\\"]?)(?P<value>[^'\\\"\\n]+)(?P=quote)\\s*$\", re.MULTILINE)\n+\n+\n+@dataclass\n+class SyncResult:\n+    expected: str\n+    previous: str\n+    changed: bool\n+\n+\n+def schedule_to_utc_cron(settings: ScheduleSettings) -> str:\n+    \"\"\"Cron expression for the schedule, shifted from its timezone into UTC.\"\"\"\n+\n+    local_cron = parse_cron(settings.as_cron())\n+    offset_minutes = _utc_offset_minutes(settings.timezone)\n+\n+    minutes = sorted(local_cron.minutes)\n+    hours = sorted(local_cron.hours)\n+    if len(minutes) > 1 or len(hours) == 24:\n+        # Sub-hourly or hourly schedules need no shift.\n+        return settings.as_cron()\n+\n+    minute = minutes[0]\n+    shifted_hours = set()\n+    for hour in hours:\n+        total = hour * 60 + minute - offset_minutes\n+        shifted_hours.add((total // 60) % 24)\n+    shifted_minute = (minute - offset_minutes) % 60\n+\n+    day_of_month = _field(local_cron.days, 1, 31)\n+    month = _field(local_cron.months, 1, 12)\n+    day_of_week = _field(local_cron.weekdays, 0, 6)\n+    hour_field = \",\".join(str(hour) for hour in sorted(shifted_hours))\n+    return f\"{shifted_minute} {hour_field} {day_of_month} {month} {day_of_week}\"\n+\n+\n+def _field(values: frozenset[int], low: int, high: int) -> str:\n+    if set(values) == set(range(low, high + 1)):\n+        return \"*\"\n+    return \",\".join(str(value) for value in sorted(values))\n+\n+\n+def _utc_offset_minutes(timezone_name: str) -> int:\n+    from datetime import datetime\n+    from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo\n+\n+    offset = datetime.now(ZoneInfo(timezone_name)).utcoffset()\n+    return int(offset.total_seconds() // 60) if offset else 0\n+\n+\n+def sync_workflow_cron(path: Path, settings: ScheduleSettings, *, check_only: bool = False) -> SyncResult:\n+    if not path.is_file():\n+        raise FileNotFoundError(f\"workflow 文件不存在: {path}\")\n+\n+    expected = schedule_to_utc_cron(settings)\n+    text = path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    match = _CRON_LINE.search(text)\n+    if match is None:\n+        raise ValueError(f\"{path} 里没有找到 '- cron:' 行\")\n+\n+    previous = match.group(\"value\").strip()\n+    if previous == expected:\n+        return SyncResult(expected=expected, previous=previous, changed=False)\n+\n+    if not check_only:\n+        replacement = f\"{match.group('indent')}'{expected}'\"\n+        path.write_text(text[: match.start()] + replacement + text[match.end() :], encoding=\"utf-8\")\n+    return SyncResult(expected=expected, previous=previous, changed=True)",

耗时 537 ms

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
All endpoints work. Now the test suite.
思考过程
I'm giving `platform` and `company` default empty-string values on `RawPosting` so `from_dict` tolerates missing fields from collectors or LLM output. I also need to fix `extract_json` so it detects whichever JSON delimiter—`{` or `[`—actually appears first in the text, rather than assuming an object.

For the rate-limit test, I'm counting the clock calls: `_respect_rate_limit` consumes two ticks and `_call_with_retries` consumes two more, so each `complete` call uses four clock reads. I'll build a fake clock as a list of timestamps that gets popped on each call, with enough zeros for the first invocation and a later timestamp for the second so the sleep math works out correctly.

耗时 1047 ms

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now tests for heuristics, analysis, config, pipeline, server and workflow.
思考过程
I'm scoring this profile for health and AI keyword matches, counting term hits like "医", "病", "算法", and "深度学习" to compute weighted category scores before moving into the skill bonus calculation.

The scoring feels too weak, so I'm refining it: capping each category at 45 with 15 points per hit, plus a skill bonus, so an on-topic post reaches around 66 while an off-topic administrative posting scores 0. I'm also checking that the default relevance threshold of 30 still makes sense and confirms the new scoring improves relevance for genuinely matching posts, then moving to update the relevance_score function.
本轮工具调用（6）

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now tests for the HTTP API and the workflow cron sync.
思考过程
I'm wondering if the 11.67s slowdown is coming from the 401 test cases, where error responses might be handling connections differently than success responses.

Let me actually run the tests with --durations to see which ones are actually slow instead of guessing.

That half-second delay is just the default poll_interval on shutdown, so I could speed up teardown in tests by passing a smaller poll_interval value to serve_forever, which is a safe test-only tweak.

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Now the frontend. Let me look at where to integrate.
思考过程
I'm planning the frontend structure: an automation.ts library for API types and client functions (fetching insights, status, triggering runs, updating schedules), plus a useAutomation hook that loads the insights data and probes the status endpoint.

I'm also designing an AutomationPanel component showing insight, schedule, last run, and controls, and thinking through how App.tsx should wire it in -- deciding to lift the automation hook up into App so the hero's "run now" button can trigger a live automation run when the API is online, falling back to a static refetch otherwise, so there's a single consistent control rather than two confusing buttons.

I want to refactor the duplicated data-loading logic into a reusable loadData function, then start defining the TypeScript types for the insights and schedule status objects that the API returns.

For the API client, I'm planning to support an optional auth token pulled from environment variables rather than hardcoding it.



I'm planning the AutomationPanel layout: a left card showing automation status (uptime, next/last run, data source, LLM provider, online state) with controls for manual runs and schedule editing, plus a right card for LLM insights with headline, summary, highlights, and skill chips—all styled to match the dark navy theme.

''\n+\n+export class AutomationApiError extends Error {}\n+\n+async function request<T>(path: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<T> {\n+  const headers = new Headers(init?.headers)\n+  if (init?.body) headers.set('Content-Type', 'application/json')\n+  if (API_TOKEN) headers.set('Authorization', `Bearer ${API_TOKEN}`)\n+\n+  const response = await fetch(`${API_BASE}${path}`, { ...init, headers })\n+  const text = await response.text()\n+  const payload = text ? 耗时 7769 ms

助手 · Cursor Grok 4.6
Frontend builds clean. Let me serve it and check the UI visually.
思考过程
Everything works, but the right card shows the fallback message since I regenerated data with `--no-llm`, leaving insights.json with a "disabled" provider and no headline. I'm weighing whether committing mock LLM text like "[mock] 离线模式生成的示例洞察" into the shipped dataset is actually worse than just showing the fallback state.

The better fix is a deterministic, data-derived fallback: a `heuristic_insights(stats)` function in enrich.py that computes a real headline, highlights from top cities/skills, and top-5 hot_skills whenever the LLM returns nothing. I'll tag insights with a `source: "llm" | "rules"` field so the UI can label which one it's showing — honest and useful without requiring an API key.

Separately, I need to investigate the 408 console error on "/api/schedule" — my handler never explicitly sends a 408, so it's likely coming from BaseHTTPRequestHandler's own timeout behavior when reading the request.

The 408 actually comes from the browser closing an idle keep-alive connection it tried to reuse—a known quirk with Python's simple HTTP server under HTTP/1.1. Since I already send Content-Length everywhere, keep-alive itself works fine; the fix is to add a `Connection: close` header on every response so the browser never holds an idle connection to reuse, which eliminates the spurious 408.

Adding the Connection: close header and close_connection flag to both response paths. I also want to verify the daily update counter behaves correctly when nothing new was added, then move on to implementing the heuristic insights fallback with a source field before regenerating the data.

I still need to update the README docs afterward, then commit, push, and update the PR. Starting with the server fix now.

思考过程

3.11+（用到 `tomllib`、`zoneinfo`、`urllib`、`http.server`）。\n+\n+```bash\n+# 1. 跑一次流水线（默认离线 mock provider，不需要 API Key）\n+cd automation\n+python -m jobsinsight run\n+\n+# 2. 看看下一次什么时候跑\n+python -m jobsinsight next-runs\n+\n+# 3. 常驻运行：到点自动执行\n+python -m jobsinsight schedule\n+\n+# 4. 或者带上 HTTP 接口一起启动（同时托管构建好的前端）\n+python -m jobsinsight serve      # http://127.0.0.1:8787/api/status\n+```\n+\n+前端：\n+\n+```bash\n+cd web\n+npm install\n+npm run dev      # http://localhost:5173\n+npm run build    # 产物在 web/dist\n+```\n+\n+Docker（调度器 + 接口 + 前端一起跑）：\n+\n+```bash\n+LLM_PROVIDER=deepseek LLM_MODEL=deepseek-chat LLM_API_KEY=sk-xxx docker compose up -d\n+```\n+\n+## 自定义运行时间\n+\n+运行时间写在 `automation/config.toml` 的 `[schedule]` 里，四种模式：\n+\n+| mode | 用到的字段 | 例子 | 说明 |\n+| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n+| `daily` | `daily_times` | `[\"00:00\", \"12:30\"]` | 每天定点，可以配多个时刻 |\n+| `cron` | `cron` | `\"30 */6 * * 1-5\"` | 标准 5 字段 cron，支持 `*/n`、区间、列表、星期名与 `@daily` 等宏 |\n+| `interval` | `interval_minutes` | `360` | 每 N 分钟跑一次，从上次运行开始算 |\n+| `manual` | — | — | 不自动运行，只接受手动或接口触发 |\n+\n+配套字段：`timezone`（默认 `Asia/Shanghai`）、`jitter_seconds`（触发时随机延迟，避开整点拥塞）、\n+`catch_up`（进程重启后补跑错过的窗口）、`run_on_start`（启动即跑一次）、`enabled`。\n+\n+三种改法，效果相同：\n+\n+```bash\n+# 改配置文件\n+vim automation/config.toml\n+\n+# 用命令行改（写入 automation/state/overrides.json，覆盖配置文件）\n+python -m jobsinsight set-schedule --at 08:30,20:30\n+python -m jobsinsight set-schedule --cron \"30 */6 * * 1-5\"\n+python -m jobsinsight set-schedule --every 90\n+python -m jobsinsight set-schedule --disable\n+\n+# 运行中通过接口改，立即生效（前端「自动化运行」面板里也能改）\n+curl -X PUT localhost:8787/api/schedule \\\n+     -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \\\n+     -d '{\"mode\":\"daily\",\"daily_times\":[\"08:30\",\"20:30\"]}'\n+```\n+\n+GitHub Actions 的 cron 只能写死在 YAML 里而且是 UTC，所以工作流用一个较粗的触发频率，\n+真正「是否该跑」交给 `run --if-due` 按你的配置判断。改完运行时间后执行\n+`python -m jobsinsight sync-cron`，会把 workflow 里的 cron 同步成对应的 UTC 时刻。\n+\n+## LLM 调用接口\n+\n+配置换一下 `provider` / `model` / `api_key` 就能切换厂商：\n+\n+```toml\n+[llm]\n+provider = \"deepseek\"        # 或 openai / moonshot / dashscope / zhipu / siliconflow / groq / ollama / anthropic / mock\n+model = \"deepseek-chat\"\n+api_key = \"${DEEPSEEK_API_KEY}\"\n+```\n+\n+- **OpenAI 兼容协议**：OpenAI、DeepSeek、Kimi/Moonshot、通义千问、智谱 GLM、SiliconFlow、\n+  Groq、vLLM、Ollama、LM Studio……自建网关填 `base_url` 即可。\n+- **Anthropic Messages API**：`provider = \"anthropic\"`。\n+- **`mock`**：内置离线实现，不联网、不需要 Key，用来先把流程跑通和跑测试。\n+\n+调用层统一提供：可重试的指数退避（含抖动）、限流最小间隔、JSON 模式与一次自动修复、\n+token 用量统计。**任何一步 LLM 失败都不会让运行失败**——会退回 `heuristics.py` 的规则解析，\n+照样产出完整数据，并把原因记进运行报告。\n+\n+LLM 在流水线里做三件事：规范化岗位字段（薪资、经验、学历、级别、技能、方向、相关度、\n+一句话摘要）、生成每日洞察、以及 `html_llm` 数据源里的网页岗位抽取。\n+\n+自定义厂商：\n+\n+```python\n+from jobsinsight.llm import register_provider, LLMProvider\n+\n+class MyProvider(LLMProvider):\n+    name = \"my-gateway\"\n+    def complete(self, request): ...\n+\n+register_provider(\"my-gateway\", lambda options: MyProvider(**options))\n+```\n+\n+## 数据源\n+\n+`[[sources]]` 可以配多个，单个来源失败不影响整次运行：\n+\n+| type | 用途 |\n+| --- | --- |\n+| `fixture` | 读本地 JSON（默认，离线可用；也用来回放已保存的抓取结果） |\n+| `json_api` | 任意 JSON 接口，字段通过 `options.field_map` 映射，支持分页与 Cookie |\n+| `html_llm` | 抓网页转成纯文本，交给 LLM 抽取结构化岗位 |\n+\n+51job / Boss直聘 / 猎聘 的搜索接口地址、参数与 Cookie 因账号而异且会变，\n+所以**不写死在代码里**，而是放在配置中（`automation/config.toml` 里有注释掉的示例）。\n+仓库默认使用 `automation/data/seed_postings.json` 作为示例数据源，开箱即可跑通。\n+\n+## HTTP 控制接口\n+\n+`python -m jobsinsight serve` 之后：\n+\n+| 方法 | 路径 | 说明 |\n+| --- | --- | --- |\n+| GET | `/api/health` | 健康检查 |\n+| GET | `/api/status` | 调度状态、LLM 配置、数据源、最近一次运行 |\n+| GET | `/api/config` | 脱敏后的完整配置 |\n+| GET | `/api/schedule` | 当前运行时间与未来 5 次触发时刻 |\n+| PUT | `/api/schedule` | 修改运行时间，立即生效并持久化 |\n+| GET | `/api/runs` · `/api/runs/latest` | 运行历史 |\n+| POST | `/api/runs` | 立即触发一次运行（`dry_run`、`limit`、`async`） |\n+| POST | `/api/llm/chat` | 透传调用 LLM（`prompt` 或 `messages`） |\n+| POST | `/api/llm/ask` | 基于当前数据问答 |\n+| GET | `/api/data/<name>.json` | 读取产出的数据文件 |\n+\n+配置了 `server.auth_token` 后，写操作需要 `Authorization: Bearer <token>`；读接口保持开放，\n+方便前端轮询状态。\n+\n+## 产出的数据\n+\n+写入 `web/public/data/`，原子写入，前端直接 fetch：\n+\n+- `jobs.json` —— 规范化后的岗位列表\n+- `stats.json` —— 平台/城市/技能/经验/学历/级别/方向/薪资分布等聚合结果，含今日新增、更新、下架\n+- `insights.json` —— 每日洞察（模型撰写或统计规则生成）+ 运行时间与上次运行信息\n+\n+运行状态在 `automation/state/`（不入库）：`state.json`（上次运行时间）、`runs.json`（运行历史）、\n+`jobs_snapshot.json`（用于算增量）、`overrides.json`（运行时改过的配置）。\n+\n+## 配置的优先级\n+\n+后面的覆盖前面的：内置默认值 → 配置文件（`config.toml` / `.json` / `.yaml`）→\n+环境变量（`JOBSINSIGHT_*`，以及字符串里的 `${VAR}` / `${VAR:-默认值}`）→\n+运行时覆盖（`automation/state/overrides.json`，由 CLI 或接口写入）。\n+\n+常用环境变量：`JOBSINSIGHT_LLM_API_KEY`、`JOBSINSIGHT_LLM_PROVIDER`、`JOBSINSIGHT_LLM_MODEL`、\n+`JOBSINSIGHT_SCHEDULE_MODE`、`JOBSINSIGHT_SCHEDULE_DAILY_TIMES`、`JOBSINSIGHT_SCHEDULE_CRON`、\n+`JOBSINSIGHT_SERVER_PORT`、`JOBSINSIGHT_SERVER_AUTH_TOKEN`。API Key 不要写进仓库。\n+\n+## 开发\n+\n+```bash\n+cd automation\n+python -m pytest          # 单元测试\n+python -m ruff check .     # lint\n+python -m ruff format .    # 格式化\n+python -m jobsinsight run --dry-run   # 跑完整流程但不写文件\n+\n+cd ../web\n+npm run lint && npm run build\n+```\n+\n+更多后端细节见 [automation/README.md](automation/README.md)，前端说明见 [web/README.md](web/README.md)。\n+\n+## 目录结构\n+\n+```\n+automation/                 自动化后端（Python，零依赖）\n+├── config.toml             运行时间、LLM、数据源、产出、接口配置\n+├── data/                   示例数据源\n+├── jobsinsight/\n+│   ├── cli.py              命令行入口\n+│   ├── config.py           配置加载与校验\n+│   ├── cron.py             5 字段 cron 解析\n+│   ├── scheduler.py        运行时间计算与调度守护\n+│   ├── collectors/         数据源（fixture / json_api / html_llm）\n+│   ├── llm/                统一 LLM 调用接口\n+│   ├── enrich.py           LLM 富化 + 洞察\n+│   ├── heuristics.py       规则兜底\n+│   ├── analysis.py         统计聚合\n+│   ├── pipeline.py         完整流水线\n+│   ├── server.py           HTTP 控制接口\n+│   └── workflow.py         同步 GitHub Actions cron\n+└── tests/                  pytest\n+\n+web/                        前端看板（React + TypeScript + Vite）\n+├── public/data/            流水线产出的 JSON\n+└── src/\n+    ├── App.tsx             看板主体\n+    ├── components/AutomationPanel.tsx   自动化状态与洞察面板\n+    ├── hooks/useAutomation.ts\n+    └── lib/automation.ts   自动化接口客户端\n+```",

3.11+ 直接跑，不需要 `pip install`。\n+\n+```bash\n+python -m jobsinsight --help\n+```\n+\n+## 命令\n+\n+| 命令 | 说明 |\n+| --- | --- |\n+| `run` | 立即执行一次流水线。`--dry-run` 不写文件，`--limit N` 限量，`--no-llm` 只用规则解析，`--if-due` 只在到点时执行 |\n+| `schedule` | 常驻运行，按设定时间自动执行（`--once` 只跑一轮，便于调试） |\n+| `serve` | 启动 HTTP 接口，默认同时启动调度器（`--no-scheduler` 只提供接口） |\n+| `next-runs` | 打印未来若干次触发时刻 |\n+| `set-schedule` | 改运行时间：`--at 08:30,20:30` / `--cron \"...\"` / `--every 90` / `--mode manual` / `--timezone` / `--enable` / `--disable` |\n+| `config` | 校验并打印当前配置（默认脱敏，`--raw` 显示明文） |\n+| `llm` | 直接调用一次 LLM，验证 provider 是否配通；`--providers` 列出内置 provider |\n+| `sync-cron` | 把运行时间同步进 GitHub Actions workflow（`--check` 只校验） |\n+| `history` | 打印运行历史 |\n+\n+## 一次运行做了什么\n+\n+1. **采集**：遍历 `[[sources]]`，按 `fingerprint`（平台+公司+岗位+城市）跨来源去重。\n+   单个来源抛错只记录进报告，不中断整次运行。\n+2. **富化**：`Enricher` 把原始岗位按 `batch_size` 分批交给 LLM，要求返回规范化字段。\n+   每个字段都会校验（枚举必须在词表内、薪资必须是合理区间），不合格就用\n+   `heuristics.py` 的规则结果。LLM 未启用、没配 Key 或调用失败时，全量走规则解析。\n+3. **过滤**：丢掉 `relevance < output.min_relevance` 的岗位（默认 30），\n+   把明显与「医药健康 + AI」无关的岗位挡在外面。\n+4. **聚合**：`analysis.build_stats()` 生成前端需要的全部统计，\n+   并与上一次快照对比得出今日新增 / 更新 / 下架。\n+5. **落盘**：原子写入 `jobs.json`、`stats.json`、`insights.json`，\n+   更新 `state/` 下的快照、运行历史与上次运行时间。\n+\n+## 关键模块\n+\n+- `cron.py` —— 自己实现的 5 字段 cron 解析器。支持 `*`、`a`、`a-b`、`a-b/n`、`*/n`、\n+  逗号列表、月份与星期名、`@daily` 等宏；day-of-month 与 day-of-week 同时受限时按\n+  cron 传统的 OR 语义处理。\n+- `scheduler.py` —— `next_run_after` / `upcoming_runs` 是纯函数（好测试也好展示），\n+  `is_due` 供 CI 的粗粒度 cron 使用，`Scheduler` 是可从其它线程 `stop()` /\n+  `trigger_now()` / `refresh()` 的守护循环。运行失败只记日志，不会终止循环。\n+- `llm/` —— `LLMProvider` 是唯一需要实现的接口；重试、限流、JSON 解析与修复、\n+  用量统计都在 `LLMClient` 里，所有 provider 共享。\n+- `heuristics.py` —— 规则解析：薪资（`25-40K·14薪`、`30-60万/年`）、经验分档、学历、\n+  级别、技能词典（别名映射到标准写法）、岗位方向、相关度打分。\n+- `store.py` —— 所有 JSON 都经临时文件 + `os.replace` 原子写入，运行中断不会留下半个文件。\n+\n+## 加一个数据源\n+\n+```python\n+from jobsinsight.collectors import Collector, register_collector\n+from jobsinsight.models import RawPosting\n+\n+class MyCollector(Collector):\n+    type = \"my-source\"\n+\n+    def collect(self):\n+        for item in fetch_somehow(self.settings.urls):\n+            yield RawPosting(\n+                source_id=item[\"id\"],\n+                platform=self.settings.platform,\n+                title=item[\"title\"],\n+                company=item[\"company\"],\n+                city=item.get(\"city\", \"\"),\n+                salary_text=item.get(\"salary\", \"\"),\n+                description=item.get(\"detail\", \"\"),\n+            )\n+\n+register_collector(\"my-source\", MyCollector)\n+```\n+\n+之后配置里写 `type = \"my-source\"` 即可。`self.context.llm` 是当前运行的 LLM 客户端\n+（可能为 `None`），`self.resolve_path()` 把相对路径按项目根目录展开。\n+\n+## 测试\n+\n+```bash\n+pip install pytest ruff       # 仅开发需要\n+python -m pytest\n+python -m ruff check . && python -m ruff format --check .\n+```\n+\n+覆盖范围：cron 解析与边界、调度与错过窗口补跑、配置层叠（文件/环境变量/运行时覆盖）、\n+LLM 重试与 JSON 修复、字段校验、规则解析、统计聚合、增量对比、HTTP 接口、CLI。\n+所有测试都不联网：网络层通过注入 transport 打桩，LLM 用内置 `mock` provider。",

  "contents": "---\nname: walkthrough-artifacts\ndescription: \"Create walkthrough artifacts (screenshots and screen recordings) that prove code changes work. Use when finishing tested changes and uploading demo evidence for the user.\"\nenvironments: [cloud]\n---\n\n# Creating & Uploading Walkthrough Artifacts\n\nWhen your changes are complete, and you have completed testing / validation, you MUST demonstrate to the user that your changes are working.\n\nIf the user explicitly requested specific manual testing, you MUST also demonstrate that you fulfilled their request.\n\nDo this with **walkthrough artifacts**. Follow this rubric, then include HTML-tag references to the artifacts inline in your final response to the user.\n\n## When to Use\n\nUse walkthrough artifacts to: (1) demonstrate that your code changes work correctly, and (2) show that any user-requested manual testing was completed.\n\n## What makes a good test artifact\n\nGood walkthrough artifacts clearly show the user that your code is fully working and any specifically requested testing was completed. Examples:\n\n- A screenshot of an implemented UI change\n- A screen recording of changes working end-to-end\n- A screen recording or screenshot showing that user-requested testing was performed\n\nThe following are examples of bad walkthrough artifacts and should NOT be uploaded:\n\n- A screen recording of a test which ultimately did not succeed. Instead of saving the video, you should fix the failure and retry!\n- Numerous screenshots of unimportant setup steps, app exploration, or failing testing steps. You may take these screenshots during testing, but do NOT upload them as artifacts.\n- Redundant walkthrough artifacts which show something that a different artifact of the same type already shows.\n- A toy artifact or fake / contrived example to \"demonstrate\" working code. NEVER create toy artifacts or fake / contrived examples.\n\nRULE: Upload the minimal set of artifacts that demonstrates your changes fully work and that all user requests were fulfilled. Videos are often helpful for showing end-to-end tests with fewer artifacts.\n\nExamples of good artifact sets:\n\n- One screenshot showing the final state after testing, and 1 or 2 screen recordings which begins right before demonstrating a working test and ends right after.\n- A few screenshots showing the before, during, and after state during your manual tests.\n- One screenshot showing key pre-test setup (e.g. altering the application settings, switching the mode, or selecting a specific model), and one screenshot or showing the after test state (or one video showing end-to-end test, minus lengthy setup steps)\n\nIf the user requested specific testing (e.g. reproducing a bug before fixing, specific setup steps, testing a feature in a particular way, etc.), you must include clearly-labeled artifacts showing that you completed the user's request(s).\n\n**Remember**: the set of uploaded artifacts must demonstrate BOTH that your code successfully implements the user's request AND that any user-requested testing was completed. Some artifacts may show that both are true, and others may show only one or the other.\n\nExamples of bad sets of artifacts to upload (DO NOT UPLOAD ARTIFACTS LIKE THIS):\n\n- A screenshot before and/or after every testing step\n- Screenshots of irrelevant application state, e.g. pre-test state, or state after the relevant portion of the application has been exited or scrolled offscreen.\n- Screenshots or videos showing failing tests. Fix these and retry! Do not upload!\n- Redundant screenshots which show the same or very similar application states.\n- Very lengthy videos which include irrelevant devex setup or application setup state. Start your recording right before the test begins!\n\n## Creating an artifact\n\n### Screenshot artifacts\n\nWhen you are using the `computerUse` subagent, it will return screenshots showing key application states. Remember these screenshot paths for future use. You should review all available artifacts at the end of your tests and decide which ones best demonstrate the correctness of your code. Show these top artifacts to the user.\n\n### Video artifacts\n\nVideo walkthroughs of working features are often the most effective way to demonstrate the correctness of your changes. When you are performing manual GUI-based testing, you MUST include a video artifact.\n\nHow to create a video artifact:\n\n1. Setup the manual test environment. This means using the `computerUse` subagent to make sure the UI is open, and navigated to the correct page or feature to demonstrate the working change(s).\n2. Begin a recording using the `RecordScreen` tool with mode set to `START_RECORDING`.\n3. Perform the manual test that demonstrates the working change(s) using the `computerUse` subagent. Provide detailed instructions for the subagent to follow.\n4. Immediately after the `computerUse` subagent responds, end the video recording. If the walkthrough was successful: save the recording to your artifacts directory by using the `RecordScreen` tool with mode set to `SAVE_RECORDING` (this will also stop the recording). If the walkthrough was unsuccessful: discard the recording by using the `RecordScreen` tool with mode set to `DISCARD_RECORDING`. Debug why the walkthrough attempt did not succeed, fix the issue, and retry.\n\nCorrect process for creating a video artifact: start recording, start `computerUse` subagent with detailed instructions, end recording.\n\n**Critical Rules:**\n\n- Always end your recording right after your manual test(s) end. If debugging, additional setup, resetting state, or other non-testing steps are required between multiple tests, split the multiple tests into multiple recordings.\n\n## Saving an artifact\n\nTo save a screenshot, copy the screenshot to your artifacts directory (the absolute path given in the system prompt under \"Creating & Uploading Walkthrough Artifacts\").
